# Final parser perf and full validation handoff - 2026-06-04

## Rourke question - theta D1/D2 rate comparison

Short answer: theta is not an apples-to-apples D1-vs-D2 transaction cost comparison. The table shows D2 peak `3683.5/s` and D1 peak `2704.7/s`, so D2 was faster by that peak metric, not slower. I did not intentionally lighten the D2 transaction in code. The run shape made theta D2 look lighter: its first `1507000` rows were unchanged `s3-first` archive reads, and current D2 policy deliberately skips normal `cache_pgcrs` writes for unchanged S3 PGCRs because the canonical archive object already exists.

D2 still did normal parser DB work for raw rows, summaries, atime/idempotent writes, characters, and profiles. The theta logs show high-volume `characterWrite*` work and mostly no-op `profileWrite*` work. Current target code also persists more than production from roughly three weeks ago in some profile/character areas, including character/profile-time persistence from the profile work. So: no, the transaction was not manually lightened; yes, the current target persists more than old production in some areas; and theta's S3-first peak should not be used as the clean worst-case D2 production write-cost comparison. Use alpha/beta/gamma/delta/epsilon or a dedicated same-source run for that.

## Asks for Rourke

- Confirm the final branch/commit is landed and no more parser/schema changes are expected during the run.
- If the new branch includes DB schema changes, point the next agent at the exact dev-safe SQL or migration path to apply before validation.
- Confirm whether theta remains dev-S3-only for this final run. Default below is dev-S3-only unless explicitly approved otherwise.

## Current handoff state

- 2026-06-06T19:21Z: Final post-production cleanup/review landed on `master` and pushed. Current `master` / `origin/master` is `6c5153d5063137ef16fc9bc4872c9b5f01f5831f` (`fix(parser): harden d2 cache repair routing`) after companion commit `204d22330e` (`test(analytics): cover closed population rollups`). The final parser/cache repair commit resolves the patch-on-patch risks from the production recovery stack: D2 cache destination decisions now reconcile Redis with stranded local cache; failed reparses retry instead of rewriting `raw_failed_pgcrs` when cache destination proof is unavailable; S3 refresh can repair an active empty existing-archive window only because D2 rollup now advances the cursor after verifying the written archive and before deleting `s3Repair` rows; future empty repair windows keep their repair rows until they can advance safely; `d2-cache-repair` now requires one canonical 1000-PGCR window, exposes `WARMIND_D2_CACHE_REPAIR_REPLACE_S3_REPAIR=true`, reports dry-run row classes/cursor state, re-checks the same cache row filename/reason in the write transaction, and avoids deleting the new normal-cache file path. Five adversarial review passes were used; all final blockers were closed. Verification passed after commit: `go test ./parser ./dev/tools/d2-cache-repair ./nexusdb ./sweeperbot -count=1`, `go build ./cmd/warmind-parser`, `go build -tags devtools ./dev/tools/d2-cache-repair`, and `git diff --check HEAD~2..HEAD`. Lesson learned: never delete sparse repair evidence until the durable cursor movement that depends on it has already succeeded, and never let cache-position proof failures become PGCR parse failures.

- 2026-06-06T16:41Z: Corrective production-recovery hardening commit created and pushed: `f82e7efe36cdd14f14de5d6513b5ddfb15a255a2` (`fix(parser): prove d2 cache repair window state`). Local `master` and `origin/master` now match this commit with a clean repo status. This supersedes `d74ab795fa` as the production recovery target. The recovery plan should pull/build this exact commit, keep parser and Cortex stopped during the bounded cache repair, run `d2-cache-repair` with `-replace-s3-repair=true` only for the known blocked window `16864554001-16864555000`, prove the window has `1000` normal cache rows and no missing raw/cache coverage, then start Cortex and verify rollup/archive advance before restarting parser.

- 2026-06-06T16:40Z: Follow-up corrective patch prepared after the `17:05Z` adversarial review findings. Local target before commit is `d74ab795fa07deae562399a4ed85aca77c0b7ae4` plus a focused dirty diff in `parser/workers.go`, `parser/helpers.go`, `parser/pipeline_test.go`, `dev/tools/d2-cache-repair`, and the two parser integrity docs. Changes: D2 failed-reparse cache routing now returns a typed decision (`normal cache`, `S3 repair`, or `wait`) based on the exact 1000-PGCR rollup window; it writes normal cache only when the active window already has normal cache rows and the exact PGCR is missing, uses S3 repair when the PGCR window is behind the rollup cursor, and rolls back/retries when it cannot prove the state. Permanent-failure local-cache saving was tightened so S3-first/S3-repair paths do not create normal cache rows for unchanged S3-owned or repair-intent failures. `dev/tools/d2-cache-repair` now defaults to treating existing `s3Repair=1` rows as present cache coverage, requires explicit `-replace-s3-repair=true` to replace those rows with normal local-cache rows, and re-locks/re-checks `raw_pgcrs.rawSha256`, mode, and period inside the write transaction before saving cache. Verification passed locally: `go test ./parser -run 'TestD2(PermFailLocalCacheSaveKeepsNormalCoverage|FailedReparseNormalCacheDecision)' -count=1`, `go test ./dev/tools/d2-cache-repair -count=1`, `go test ./dev/tools/d2-cache-repair ./bungie ./nexusdb ./parser ./sweeperbot ./d1parser ./d1cortex -count=1`, `go build ./cmd/warmind-parser`, and `go build -tags devtools ./dev/tools/d2-cache-repair`. This is still not Stage 3 validation; it is a production-recovery hardening patch before the bounded cache repair.

- 2026-06-05T15:09Z: PGCR S3 production cutover plan clarified. Old D1/D2 S3 PGCR archives must be treated as invalid input because they are both corrupt and in the legacy/wrong shape; do not use them as `s3-first` source truth and do not try to repair them in place. The production cutover should create a new canonical archive generation, preferably a new prefix or bucket/prefix, and set both read `s3-prefix` and `s3-write-prefix` to that clean generation until it is independently verified. Normal parser startup should begin with `parser.source-policy=auto` or `bungie-only` so the parser fetches Bungie/live DB truth and the rollup/Cortex writes new `pgcr-jsonl-zstd-v1` canonical batches from fresh local cache rows. `s3-first` should only be enabled after the new prefix has complete verified canonical windows for the ranges the parser will read. A full historical S3 replacement cannot be produced from the old S3 archives or DB metadata alone; any old-window rebuild needs exact raw PGCR bytes from local cache if present or fresh Bungie refetch/backfill, with hash/source checks and no validation-derived truth. No production DB/S3 was touched.

- 2026-06-05T14:35Z: Production-readiness schema review found one blocker before deploying the parser branch to production. The branch adds D2/Nexus migration `nexusdb/migrations/20260605_0001_pgcr_parser_shape.sql` and D1 migrations `0012_drop_failed_terminal_flag.sql` / `0013_cache_repair_columns.sql`. New parser code is not backward-compatible with the old schema because it inserts/selects new columns directly. Most D2 changes are additive, but the current D2 migration adds `raw_pgcrs.status` with default `parsed` and does not convert legacy failed coverage markers (`raw_pgcrs.mode=255`, especially rows also present in `raw_failed_pgcrs`) to `status='parse_failed'`. That would make old failed markers look parsed to the new code and can block successful error reparse replacement, because the new replacement path only deletes non-parsed raw markers. Before production, patch the D2 migration or add a separate migration/backfill that marks existing failed raw markers as `parse_failed` before starting the new parser. Normal parser cutover does not require inventing a special PGCR stop id, but production needs a controlled schema/app cutover with parser/rollup writers stopped or fenced, schema prechecks recorded, D2 failed-marker conversion applied, D1 terminal-column precheck handled if D1 production data exists, and then new parser/rollup/Cortex started from DB/Redis truth. No production DB/S3 was touched.

Added D2 parser/Cortex metrics/logging for ignored future high-water and skipped future population events. Focused tests passed: `go test ./popanalytics ./memcache ./sweeperbot -count=1`. - 2026-06-04T08:12Z: Local bug-fix patch applied for the stopped active-job loss path and the two adjacent safety/visibility holes. `bungie/client.go` now sets `http.Client.Timeout` from the configured Bungie timeout for source-bound and default clients, so active PGCR HTTP fetches cannot hang forever after connect. `parser/workers.go` now treats any panic before DB commit, including pre-transaction panics, as `ParserWorkerPanic` so the normal retry/fail path sees it instead of counting it as success. `parser/parser.go` now logs every deferred stopped PGCR ID while still keeping the minimum deferred ID for Redis status. - 2026-06-04T08:05Z: Root cause identified for the three interior D2 alpha holes. The stopped parser still had `activeJobs=3`, matching the three missing interior PGCRs, while `jobQueue=0`, `retryQueue=0`, and `dbQueue=0`; those IDs were still live in parser-owned work, not durably finished. The blocking path is the Bungie HTTP client: `getHttpClient` applies the configured timeout only to the dialer and never sets `http.Client.Timeout`, so a PGCR fetch can hang forever after connection setup. On the source-bound EC2 parser, that leaves active jobs with no raw/cache/failed/log result; a later forced process stop drops the IDs. This also explains why the missing IDs had no exact parser line and why Bungie live fetch now succeeds. Planned fix: set `http.Client.Timeout` from `bungie.http-timeout` for all Bungie clients, add a narrow regression test, and keep the existing parser retry/deferred-stop path responsible for durable outcome once a bounded fetch returns. No validation will resume until this and the already found safety/logging fixes pass locally and on EC2.

Alpha normal PGCR fetches came from live Bungie, not S3 (`source_policy=auto`, `s3FetchHit=0`), and sampled missing PGCRs fetched cleanly from Bungie with matching instance ids. The failure was inside parser job handling: Bungie fetch pressure/rate-limit responses set the shared source-pause flag; a normal job already pulled from the work queue during that pause returned the parser's pause error, and the old `processFetchedD2Job` path marked the job finished without raw/cache/failed output, retry, deferred record, or per-id log. Panic was checked and does not fit alpha: no panic/`ParserWorkerPanic`/retry-skipped/DB-handoff-stopped log evidence was present, and DB-worker errors before commit route to retry. Local patch changes pause-hit jobs to schedule a normal retry and log `D2_FETCH_PAUSE_REQUEUED`; it also stops worker scale-down from dropping a retry enqueue after a PGCR has already been consumed. Focused local tests passed with `GOCACHE=/private/tmp/warmind-gocache-final-f105 go test ./parser -run 'TestD2SourcePauseFetchRequeuesWithoutDurableOutcome|TestD2PGCRNotFoundUsesNormalRetryQueue|TestD2FetchPressurePauseDoesNotLookLikeRealtimeEdge|TestD2RetryEnqueueIgnoresWorkerStopWhenQueueIsFull|TestD2RetryEnqueueSkipsAfterGlobalStop' -count=1`, and `git diff --check` passed. - 2026-06-04T05:34Z: Root-caused and repaired a dev Redis Cluster side-effect during the soak. Parser retries were repeatedly seeing `dial tcp 172.20.0.5:0` from go-redis cluster discovery. The cluster masters reported normal `:6379` slots, but `redis-cluster-5` had stale persisted `nodes.conf`/slot gossip: `redis-cluster-2` appeared as port `0` and `redis-cluster-5` advertised an old IP. Because the app seeds the Redis cluster client with all six dev nodes, a parser client could learn the bad slot map from that replica. Dev-only repair ran `CLUSTER MEET` from each Redis cluster node to all six current container IPs, after which `redis-cluster-5 CLUSTER SLOTS` reported `redis-cluster-2 6379` and the correct replica IP. Recreated parser/Cortex so their Redis clients dropped the poisoned slot cache; then cleared the stale dev-only `warmind:parser:singleton:d2` key left by the forced parser recreate. The clean post-repair log sample had no `172.20.0.5:0`, schema, panic, fatal, or S3 repair errors; parser's remaining `last_error` was Bungie API throttle `#55`, consistent with the known second parser/key contention. Follow-up candidate: harden `dev/scripts/redis-cluster-init.sh` so an already-OK cluster refreshes node gossip before declaring itself healthy, or seed dev app clients with masters only. No code or production data changed in this repair.
